for a preliminary ruling on the interpretation of each court The court has jurisdiction to refer the question to the Court of Justice concerning the interpretation of Community law, if it considers it necessary to resolve the dispute before it.
, contrary to that court whose decision cannot be challenged by means of appeal under national law, it is essentially required to submit such a question to the Court unless there is a case-law in the area (and any new framework does not establish the actual doubts as to the possibility of applying that case-law) or is the correct way of interpreting Community rules is entirely clear.
, in particular, where the case-law of the Court of Justice is sufficiently clear, the Court of First Instance may decide on the correct interpretation of Community law and its application to the factual findings.
for a preliminary ruling may, however, prove in a suitable stage of the procedure as particularly useful if it is a new question of interpretation which is of general importance for the uniform application of Community law in the Union or where the current case-law does not appear to be applicable to a completely new factual framework.
, the national court is entitled to explain what the interpretation required is necessary to interpret its judgment.
for a preliminary ruling on the assessment Where national courts have the opportunity to refuse to reject the pleas in law relating to invalidity which are applied before them, the possibility to declare Community act invalid falls within the exclusive jurisdiction of the Court of Justice.
Each national court must therefore refer to the Court a request for a preliminary ruling if it has doubts about the validity of the Community act, indicating the reasons for which it considers that Community act could be vitiated by invalidity.
, however, if the national court has serious doubts about the validity of the Community act on which national act is based, it may exceptionally suspend temporarily the implementation of that act or in that regard, adopt any other precautionary measure.
is then obliged to ask the question concerning the validity of the Court and to state reasons why it considers that the Community act is not valid.
, at the time of the application for a preliminary ruling, the national court may submit a request for a preliminary ruling once it finds that a decision on the question or question of interpretation or validity is necessary to enable the judgment to be delivered; it is a national court which may best assess the procedure at which stage of the procedure is to be brought.
however, it is desirable that the decision to refer the question referred for a preliminary ruling is adopted at the stage of the procedure where the court is able to define the factual and legal framework of the problem, so that the Court should have any evidence necessary to verify that Community law in the main proceedings is applicable.
may also be shown in order to properly exercise justice, in order to ask the question referred for a preliminary ruling after the contradictory discussion of the case.
: In the form of a reference for a preliminary ruling from the decision of the national court, a preliminary ruling may be adopted in any form which national law admits for the case of procedural issues.

Article 41 of Regulation (EU) No 1380/2013 of the European Parliament and of the Council ( 2 ) is financial assistance from the European Maritime and Fisheries Fund (ENRF) subject to compliance with the CRP rules by the Member States.
-related rules of the CRP Member States may result in interruptions or suspension of payments or financial corrections of the Union financial assistance provided under the CRP.
Article 83(1) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 of the European Parliament and of the Council ( 3 ) provides for the conditions under which the suspension of payment periods may be imposed and Article 142(1) of that regulation may be imposed.
Article 2 of the Rules of Procedure in the framework of the CRP may provide specific rules for ENRF to provide for specific reasons for interrupting and suspending payments.
In order to protect the financial interests of the Union and its taxpayers, where the Member State failed to fulfil its obligations under the CRP or where the Commission has evidence indicating that such failure is not fulfilled, the Commission has the possibility to suspend payment deadlines under Article 100 of Regulation (EU) No 508/2014.
, in addition to the discontinuation of payment periods and in order to avoid the risk of payment of unsuitable expenditure, the Commission may suspend, in the event of a serious failure to comply with the CRP rules in accordance with Article 101 of Regulation (EU) No 508/2014 payment.
financial consequences for penalties imposed on Member States should be proportionate to the nature, gravity, duration and repeat of non-compliance with rules.

ic agents should be used with caution in patients with a potential risk of increased bleeding after trauma, operation or as a result of other pathological conditions and, if co-administration of clopidogrel and ASA, heparin inhibitors, glycoprotein IIb/IIIa inhibitors or nonsteroid antiflogic (NSAID) inhibitors, including cox-2 inhibitors or selective serotonin retroactin recovery inhibitors (SSRI).
patients should be carefully monitored to see if any signs of bleeding including occult bleeding, especially during the first weeks of treatment and/or intravascular cardiological performance or surgery should be observed.
concomitant administration of clopidogrel with oral anticoagulants is not recommended, since bleeding intensity may increase (see section 4.5).
In the event that the patient is required to undergo the planned operation and antiagregation effect is not temporarily desirable, clopidogrel should be discontinued 7 days before the performance.
patients should notify doctors prior to planning any surgery or other pharmacotherapy to dental physicians that they are taking clopidogrel before planning any surgery or other pharmacotherapy.
-designopidogrel prolongs bleeding periods and should therefore be administered to patients with tendency to haemorrhage (particularly gastrointestinal and intraocular) cautiously.
patients should be informed that if they are taking clopidogrel (about or in combination with ASA), the bleeding constraint could take longer than usual and that they should inform their doctor about any unusual bleeding (place of occurrence or length).
Thrombocytopenic Purpura (TTTP) Thrombocytopenic Purpura (TTTP) was reported very rarely after clopidogrel use, sometimes after short exposure.
is characterised by thrombocytopenia and microangiopathic haemolytic anaemia associated either with neurological findings, renal dysfunction or fever.
TTP is potentially fatal condition requiring uninterrupted treatment including plasma spawning.
-modified haemophilia Following clopidogrel use, haemophilia has been reported.
In cases where a unique occurrence of prolonged activated partial thromboplastin time (APTT) with bleeding or without bleeding, the possibility of acquired haemophilia is required to take account of the possibility of hemophilia.
patients with confirmed diagnosis obtained by haemophilia should be treated with a specialized doctor and clopidogrel administration should be discontinued.
stroke Given the lack of data, clopidogrel cannot be recommended during the first 7 days after acute ischaemic stroke.

, on the basis of the correct analysis, the Court of First Instance excluded any similarity between marks and 53 to 57 of the judgment under appeal, that, despite the good name of earlier marks and the identity of the goods covered by the marks in question, there is no likelihood of confusion between those marks.
: 59 The third part of the first ground of appeal must therefore be declared unfounded.
, in those circumstances, the first ground of appeal must be rejected.
: Second ground of appeal Arguments of the parties 61 By its second ground of appeal, the appellant alleges that it infringed Article 8(5) of Regulation No 40/94, when it did not examine the reputation of the earlier marks under that provision.
, in paragraph 15 of the judgment under appeal, the Court of First Instance found that the appellant claims that it had applied a single plea alleging infringement of Article 8(1)(b) of Regulation No 40/94, whereas the action was also explicitly based on infringement of Article 8(5) of that regulation.
63 The Court of First Instance erred in law in finding that, since the marks are not similar, it is not necessary to examine the good name of the earlier marks.
: The fact that the earlier mark of Calvin Klein is generally known, should be taken into account in the appellant for the purposes of determining whether more extensive protection should be granted to it.
: Good name of the earlier mark must be assessed in assessing similarity between marks and not after similarity has been shown.
, in that regard, the appellant submits that if the prolonged mark cK would be protected only if the third parties ‘cK’ would have used the same way of graphic formulation, it would mean that the trade mark of the CK with a good name would be granted a level of protection which is practically lower than the level of protection which would have been used if it were not generally known.
